1: #!/bin/bash
2: # GATE 6 -- no distress markers in the boot logs.
3: #
4: # Cheap, and it has caught real regressions: round 40 found that #265's own diagnostic
5: # flooded the log on a healthy boot, and that #261 was listing a subsystem it had not
6: # actually armed. Neither changed a pass/fail count -- both showed up only as marker counts.
7: #
8: # READS THE RAW PTY LOGS ON PURPOSE. fatal() and debugmsg() output is not guest console
9: # output; it goes straight to stdout and interleaves into the pty stream, and the arc
10: # screen reconstruction that gate 4 needs for GUEST tokens actively garbles it. Measured:
11: # '{ asc: data in' reads 0 from the arc screen rebuild and 5 from the raw log.
12: #
13: # TWO WAYS THIS GATE USED TO PASS WITHOUT PROVING ANYTHING, both now closed:
14: #
15: #  * EMPTY LOGS. It checked only that the files existed. `: > /tmp/pD.log` made 20 checks
16: #    go green -- two empty files certifying a clean boot. There is now a POSITIVE CONTROL:
17: #    each log must be substantial AND contain the guest's own uid=0(root), so absence of
18: #    distress is only counted when presence of a real boot is proved.
19: #  * STALE LOGS. Everything lives in /tmp and survives until reboot, so running this gate
20: #    alone -- or after gate 4 skipped -- graded yesterday's logs, produced by a binary that
21: #    no longer exists. Gate 4 now deletes them first and copies them into $LOGDIR when it
22: #    finishes; this gate reads THOSE copies, which only exist if gate 4 actually ran.
23: set -u
24: HERE=$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)
25: . "$HERE/lib.sh"
26: 
27: gate_begin "log-hygiene"
28: 
29: # ---- static wiring: the readiness-predicate ratchet ---------------------
30: #
31: #  DELIBERATELY BEFORE the log check below, so a missing log can never SKIP
32: #  it.  This gate had no source assertions at all until now -- it graded logs
33: #  and nothing else -- which is why the defect it guards could spread unseen.
34: #
35: #  THE DEFECT IT FREEZES: a readiness predicate matching a BARE ">" is
36: #  satisfied by the guest's own register dump, whose FIRST line is the
37: #  symbol line "  <...>" -- and that line ends in ">".  Line-buffered on a
38: #  pty, each line is its own flush, so a reader waking between line 1 and
39: #  line 2 returns SATISFIED WITH THE REGISTERS UNREAD.  Healthy host,
40: #  microseconds wide, and armed on every CPU family whose dump starts that
41: #  way (MIPS, PPC, SH, m88k all do).
42: #
43: #  THE CONVERSION HAS LANDED (#392), so EXPECT_BARE is now 0 and this is a
44: #  RATCHET AGAINST REGRESSION rather than against spread.  All 15 sites now take
45: #  a fresh mark before the write, require the FULL prompt in that slice, and
46: #  require the command's own echo first.
47: #
48: #  The converted predicate deliberately keeps the LITERAL endswith("GXemul>")
49: #  spelling, which is the idiom the ARM probes already use (arm_idle_probe.py:194).
50: #  A reviewer proposed a shared helper holding the prompt in a constant or a
51: #  regex; either would have broken this gate, though -- as a pass-2 seat pointed
52: #  out -- NOT by the same route, and the first draft of this comment conflated
53: #  them:
54: #      endswith(PROMPT)  -> counted, unrecognised -> fails the `unknown` check;
55: #      re.search(...)    -> not an endswith at all, so `unknown` never moves --
56: #                           it fails the anchored-site COUNT below instead.
57: #  Matching the house idiom kept the ratchet working with no redesign, which is
58: #  worth remembering the next time a "cleaner" abstraction is proposed for
59: #  something a static check is watching.
60: #
61: #  Exact equality, not a ceiling, and on purpose: a "<=" check would let the
62: #  number fall silently, and a silent fall is how you lose track of whether
63: #  the conversion actually happened.  Any movement in either direction must
64: #  be a deliberate edit here.
65: #  COUNTING IT PROPERLY, after a review pass named three ways to walk around a
66: #  naive grep: the single-quoted spelling, a variable or helper holding the
67: #  prompt, and probes living in a subdirectory the flat glob never visits.
68: #  A ratchet you can step around is worse than no ratchet, because it reads as
69: #  protection.  So:
70: #    - find RECURSIVELY, not "$HERE"/*.py;
71: #    - accept either quote style;
72: #    - and make the check CLOSED-WORLD: every .endswith() in the tree must use
73: #      one of the two KNOWN literals.  A third spelling -- endswith(PROMPT),
74: #      say -- is unknown, and unknown fails.  A helper module under regress/
75: #      is scanned like a probe: an endswith( line there with a known literal
76: #      is not "unknown" (the bare one still fails the bare row), and one with
77: #      an unrecognised argument fails "unknown".  What no count here
78: #      sees is WHICH probe a helper's test serves: a probe whose ONLY test
79: #      moved into a helper is listed promptless and the names row reds
80: #      (fail-closed), while a probe that keeps one call of its own can move
81: #      the rest into a helper with every row green (hygienecensusholes n3k,
82: #      n3k2; this said a helper's line was unknown until the census F3 pass,
83: #      codex and grok).  That converts "I grepped for the bad thing" into "I
84: #      enumerated everything and recognised all of it" -- for the spellings
85: #      these counts can SEE.
86: #      (*** 2026-09-23: this said it was "the only form that cannot be evaded
87: #      by inventing a new way to write it".  FALSE: every TEXTUAL count here
88: #      (bare, allends, full, unknown -- the token classifier is not one of
89: #      them) is LINE-based and keys on `endswith(`, so a space before the paren
90: #      (`endswith (">")`) or a backslash continuation between the name and
91: #      the paren removes a call from all of them (both measured on copies,
92: #      controls_f2 e_e9 and controls_c5 t_bsl).  Filed as hygienecensusholes
93: #      e9, ranked first; the census F3 pass widened it from one spelling to
94: #      this LINE-based class (glm, agy, the kimi substitute).  And the
95: #      comment filter drops every PHYSICAL line that begins with `#`: code on
96: #      such a line (after a closing triple quote, or in an f-string field) and
97: #      a whole file saved with CR-only line endings (grep then sees one line,
98: #      beginning `#!`) escape too -- measured, the census F4 pass (the Opus
99: #      seat: hash_line, cr_only), filed with e10.  The census is closed-world
100: #      over the literal spellings on the physical lines it keeps, not over
101: #      the method. ***)
102: EXPECT_BARE=0
103: EXPECT_UNKNOWN=0
104: #  CODE ONLY.  The first version of this check counted a line of PROSE -- a
105: #  comment in arm_flags_probe.py that discusses endswith() -- and reported an
106: #  unrecognised spelling that does not exist.  A check that fires on its own
107: #  documentation trains the reader to ignore it, so full-line comments are
108: #  stripped before anything is counted.  (Known limit, stated rather than
109: #  hidden: an endswith() sitting after code on the same line as a trailing
110: #  comment is still counted as code, which is the safe direction.)
111: #  readiness_predicate_test.py is EXCLUDED here and pinned separately below.
112: #  It is the offline truth table for #392, so it necessarily CONTAINS the two
113: #  broken spellings -- demonstrating them is its whole job. Counting them with
114: #  the live sites would make the ratchet permanently red.
115: #
116: #  This is a FAIL-CLOSED exception, not a hole: the file is named exactly, and
117: #  its own contents are asserted below, so it cannot quietly stop testing what it
118: #  claims to test. A loose `grep -v test` would have been the wrong shape -- it
119: #  would silently exempt any future file with "test" in its name.
120: #  *** SCOPE, MEASURED 2026-08-29: `regress/artifacts/` IS PRESERVED EVIDENCE, NOT LIVE
121: #  HARNESS CODE, and leaving it in scope was a defect in BOTH directions. ***
122: #
123: #  *** THE NUMBERS THAT WERE HERE WENT STALE INSIDE THIS VERY DIFF, so they are
124: #  replaced by their DERIVERS.  An earlier draft froze 138 / 59 / 59; this round then
125: #  added wdc_latch_census.py and the true values became 139 / 60 / 60 -- a frozen count
126: #  in a comment ABOUT counting, wrong before the commit that wrote it had landed.
127: #  Caught by the codex seat. ***
128: #
129: #      find . -name "*.py" | wc -l                             the OLD recursive scope
130: #      find . -name "*.py" -not -path "*/artifacts/*" | wc -l  the NEW one
131: #      ls *.py | wc -l                                         the flat glob
132: #      grep -l "pty.fork" artifacts/*/*.py artifacts/*/*/*.py  the invisible copies
133: #
134: #  THE LOAD-BEARING FACT IS THE EQUALITY, NOT THE VALUES: recursive-minus-artifacts
135: #  equals the flat glob, which is what makes this the right exclusion -- it ALIGNS the
136: #  censuses instead of moving any of them.  Re-derive rather than quote; if the two
137: #  ever differ, that difference is the finding.
138: #
139: #  Two censuses recursed while a third used a flat glob -- which is evasion route #3
140: #  that this file condemns ninety lines above.  TEN pty-forking promptless probes sat
141: #  under artifacts/, invisible to the denominator, to the promptless pin and to the
142: #  comm -13 offender-naming: invisible to the entire mechanism built to find them.
143: #
144: #  AND THE HAZARD WAS SCHEDULED IN THE OTHER DIRECTION.  CLAUDE.md MANDATES frozen
145: #  probe copies for a comparison run; copying one converted probe into artifacts/
146: #  takes conv_anchor 23 -> 24 and reds this gate -- so the next round to freeze a
147: #  probe would have gone red FOR OBEYING THE CARRIER.
148: #
149: #  RECURSION IS KEPT so a genuinely new subdirectory is still scanned; only the
150: #  evidence archive is excluded.  Measured a NO-OP on every pinned count today.
151: py_code() { find "$HERE" -name '*.py' -type f ! -path "$HERE/artifacts/*" \
152:                  ! -name 'readiness_predicate_test.py' -print0 2>/dev/null | \
153:             xargs -0 grep -h "endswith(" 2>/dev/null | grep -v '^[[:space:]]*#'; }
154: #  -o, so these count OCCURRENCES and not LINES.  With -c a single line holding
155: #  two different spellings counts once in each of the three totals and drives
156: #  `unknown` NEGATIVE -- a subtraction is only meaningful if all three terms
157: #  count the same kind of thing.
158: bare=$(py_code | grep -o "endswith([\"']>[\"'])" | wc -l)
159: #  Every endswith( in code, minus the two we recognise: the bare prompt above
160: #  and the correct full prompt.  Anything left is a spelling nobody reviewed --
161: #  including endswith(PROMPT), wherever it sits under regress/ (a helper module included).
162: allends=$(py_code | grep -o "endswith(" | wc -l)
163: full=$(py_code | grep -o "endswith([\"']GXemul>[\"'])" | wc -l)
164: unknown=$((allends - bare - full))
165: check "readiness: bare-prompt sites frozen (#37)" "$bare" "$EXPECT_BARE"
166: check "readiness: no unrecognised endswith spelling" "$unknown" "$EXPECT_UNKNOWN"
167: 
168: #  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
169: #  *** THE THREE COUNTS ABOVE ARE CLOSED-WORLD AND STRUCTURALLY CANNOT SEE A
170: #  PROBE WITH NO PROMPT DETECTION AT ALL. ***  py_code() concatenates every
171: #  `endswith(` line across the tree, so a file containing ZERO of them
172: #  contributes to no counter and passes invisibly.  A ratchet built to catch
173: #  probes with BAD prompt detection is blind to probes with NONE -- which is
174: #  the larger set: SEVEN of the 33 pty-forking probes have no prompt
175: #  detection whatever.
176: #
177: #  The fix is the one this project applies to every phantom-zero: ENUMERATE THE
178: #  DENOMINATOR FIRST.  Count the pty-forking probes, then the promptless subset,
179: #  and compare that subset against an explicit list.
180: #
181: #  *** IT IS A RATCHET, NOT A REJECT, AND THAT IS NOT A COMPROMISE. ***  EVERY
182: #  current probe is promptless, so "reject any file lacking a predicate" would
183: #  redden the whole battery PERMANENTLY -- a red no edit can clear, which this
184: #  project has already paid for once and which destroys the credibility of every
185: #  other row in the run.  The grandfathered set is named exactly, so it cannot
186: #  quietly grow; a NEW promptless probe fails, and converting one is a
187: #  ratchet-down that must edit this list.
188: #
189: #  Provenance: a codex seat named the enumerate-first shape on the
190: #  probetimebased panel; the ratchet-not-reject refinement is the adjudication's,
191: #  after it checked what a reject-all would do to the current set.
192: #  *** BOTH PINS BELOW WERE RED AT HEAD UNTIL 2026-08-27, FOR THE SAME CAUSE, AND NOBODY

#!/bin/bash
# census F6d controls (c6d), WSL cases, each on F6d (SEC now) and F6 (the files the F5 pass measured); /tmp copies only.
# PREDICTIONS.txt (controls_c6b/) was written first.
set -u
SEC=/mnt/c/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC
S5=/mnt/c/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/census0923
OUT=$SEC/regress/artifacts/hygienecensus_20260923/controls_c6d
W=/tmp/gxcensus_c6d; rm -rf "$W"; mkdir -p "$W"
T=$OUT/TABLE_new.txt; : > "$T"
RUL="2026-09-23: owner ruling for this control case only"
EW="ends""with"
mk() {  # $1 case, $2 F6d|F6 -> prints the tree root
  local d=$W/$1_$2; rm -rf "$d"; mkdir -p "$d/GXEMUL-SEC/regress" "$d/GXEMUL-SEC/tools"
  (cd "$SEC/regress" && find . -maxdepth 1 -type f -exec cp -p {} "$d/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/" \;)
  if [ "$2" = F6d ]; then cp -p "$SEC/tools/derive_hygiene_pins.sh" "$d/GXEMUL-SEC/tools/"
  else cp -p "$S5/derive_hygiene_pins.f6.sh" "$d/GXEMUL-SEC/tools/derive_hygiene_pins.sh"; cp -p "$S5/gate_hygiene.f6.sh" "$d/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/gate_hygiene.sh"; fi
  echo "$d"; }
drv() { local d=$1; shift; ( cd "$d/GXEMUL-SEC/regress" && GXROOT="$d" bash "$d/GXEMUL-SEC/tools/derive_hygiene_pins.sh" "$@" ) > "$W/last.out" 2>&1; DRC=$?; }
first() { grep -E '^(REFUSED|WRITTEN|DERIVE_FAIL|GRANDFATHERED|RELEASED|DERIVE_DONE)' "$W/last.out" | head -1 | cut -c1-120; }
pin() { grep -E "^$2=" "$1/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/gate_hygiene.sh" | head -1; }
gfn() { sed -n '/^GRANDFATHERED_PROMPTLESS="/,/"[[:space:]]*$/p' "$1/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/gate_hygiene.sh" | tr ' "' '\n\n' | grep -c '\.py$'; }
sect() { sed -n "/^$2/,/^[A-Z]/p" "$1" | grep -E '^  ' | sed 's/^  //' | tr '\n' ' '; }
gate() {  # $1 tree -> GF (readiness FAIL count), GN (readiness rows seen), GL (printed new list)
  local d=$1; mkdir -p "$d/logs"
  ( cd "$d/GXEMUL-SEC/regress" && LOGDIR="$d/logs" bash ./gate_hygiene.sh ) > "$W/gate.out" 2>&1
  sed 's/\x1b\[[0-9;]*m//g' "$W/gate.out" | grep -E '^\s*(FAIL|ok)' | grep -E 'readiness|whole-buffer' > "$W/gate.rows"
  GN=$(wc -l < "$W/gate.rows"); GF=$(grep -cE '^\s*FAIL' "$W/gate.rows")
  GL=$(grep -E '^    [^ ].*\.py$' "$W/gate.out" | sed 's/^ *//' | tr '\n' ' '); }
{ echo "controls_c6d -- $(date '+%F %T')  F6d deriver $(md5sum $SEC/tools/derive_hygiene_pins.sh | cut -c1-8) gate $(md5sum $SEC/regress/gate_hygiene.sh | cut -c1-8) census_tokens $(md5sum $SEC/regress/census_tokens.py | cut -c1-8)  F6 deriver $(md5sum $S5/derive_hygiene_pins.f6.sh | cut -c1-8) gate $(md5sum $S5/gate_hygiene.f6.sh | cut -c1-8)"
  echo "PREDICTIONS.txt mtime: $(stat -c '%y' "$OUT/PREDICTIONS.txt" | cut -c1-19)"; } | tee "$OUT/SUMMARY_new.txt"
for V in F6d F6; do
  # t_wfcolon2 -- the Opus seat's P2 on F6
  d=$(mk t_wfcolon2 $V); R=$d/GXEMUL-SEC/regress; cp "$R/arm_flags_probe.py" "$R/zz:9 x.py"
  drv "$d" --write --ruling "$RUL"; cp "$W/last.out" "$OUT/t_wfcolon2_$V.out"; gate "$d"
  echo "t_wfcolon2   $V rc=$DRC  $(first)  | $(pin "$d" EXPECT_WHOLE_FULL) $(pin "$d" WHOLE_FULL_FILES)  gate $GF FAIL of $GN" >> "$T"
  # t_wfcolon3 -- the residue filed LOW (grok, the F5 pass, on the F6 draft)
  d=$(mk t_wfcolon3 $V); R=$d/GXEMUL-SEC/regress; cp "$R/arm_flags_probe.py" "$R/arm_bdt_probe.py:9 x.py"; printf '# a plain file\n' > "$R/x.py"
  drv "$d" --write --ruling "$RUL"; cp "$W/last.out" "$OUT/t_wfcolon3_$V.out"; gate "$d"
  echo "t_wfcolon3   $V rc=$DRC  $(first)  | $(pin "$d" EXPECT_WHOLE_FULL) $(pin "$d" WHOLE_FULL_FILES)  gate $GF FAIL of $GN" >> "$T"
  # t_k14release -- a match class pattern credited as a readiness call, then --release
  d=$(mk t_k14release $V); R=$d/GXEMUL-SEC/regress
  printf '\n\ndef _k14(x):\n    match x:\n        case K.%s("GXemul>"):\n            pass\n' "$EW" >> "$R/gdrom_cond_probe.py"
  drv "$d"; G1=$(sect "$W/last.out" 'GRANDFATHERED BUT'); cp "$W/last.out" "$OUT/t_k14release_${V}_report.out"
  drv "$d" --release gdrom_cond_probe.py; cp "$W/last.out" "$OUT/t_k14release_$V.out"
  echo "t_k14release $V GONE: [$G1]  --release rc=$DRC  $(first)  | list $(gfn "$d") names" >> "$T"
  # t_partial -- the classifier dies after ten input lines (the Opus seat's k_partial)
  d=$(mk t_partial $V); R=$d/GXEMUL-SEC/regress
  python3 - "$R/census_tokens.py" <<'PY'
import io, sys
p = sys.argv[1]; s = io.open(p, encoding="utf-8").read()
old = "    for line in sys.stdin.read().split(\"\\n\"):"
assert s.count(old) == 1, s.count(old)
new = "    _n = [0]\n    for line in sys.stdin.read().split(\"\\n\"):\n        _n[0] += 1\n        if _n[0] > 10: raise RuntimeError(\"simulated classifier crash\")"
io.open(p, "w", encoding="utf-8").write(s.replace(old, new))
PY
  drv "$d"; RRC=$DRC; G1=$(sect "$W/last.out" 'GRANDFATHERED BUT' | wc -w); cp "$W/last.out" "$OUT/t_partial_${V}_report.out"
  drv "$d" --release gdrom_cond_probe.py; cp "$W/last.out" "$OUT/t_partial_$V.out"
  echo "t_partial    $V report rc=$RRC GONE listed $G1  --release rc=$DRC  $(first)  | list $(gfn "$d") names" >> "$T"
  # t_globname -- a glob character in a TREE file name (the Opus seat's P7)
  d=$(mk t_globname $V); R=$d/GXEMUL-SEC/regress; cp "$R/pcc2_width_probe.py" "$R/zz*.py"; printf '# a plain file, not a probe\n' > "$R/zz_other.py"
  drv "$d"; cp "$W/last.out" "$OUT/t_globname_$V.out"
  NL=$(sect "$W/last.out" 'NEW PROMPTLESS'); UZ=$(sect "$W/last.out" 'UNTRACKED' | tr ' ' '\n' | grep -E '^zz' | tr '\n' ' '); gate "$d"
  echo "t_globname   $V rc=$DRC  NEW printed [$NL]  UNTR zz* printed [$UZ]  gate $GF FAIL of $GN, printed [$GL]" >> "$T"
  # t_rlf / t_rctl -- a LINE FEED / a control byte inside a ruling (glm, minimax)
  d=$(mk t_rlf $V); cp -p "$d/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/gate_hygiene.sh" "$W/b"
  drv "$d" --write --ruling $'2026-09-23: sixteen letters of reason\nrm -rf x'; cp "$W/last.out" "$OUT/t_rlf_$V.out"
  echo "t_rlf        $V rc=$DRC  $(first)  gate $(cmp -s "$W/b" "$d/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/gate_hygiene.sh" && echo unchanged || echo CHANGED)" >> "$T"
  drv "$d" --write --ruling $'2026-09-23: sixteen letters of reason\x01more'; cp "$W/last.out" "$OUT/t_rctl_$V.out"
  echo "t_rctl       $V rc=$DRC  $(first)  gate $(cmp -s "$W/b" "$d/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/gate_hygiene.sh" && echo unchanged || echo CHANGED)" >> "$T"
  # t_pyopt0 / t_pyopt1 -- the writer's multiplicity check is an assert (codex, the F5 pass)
  for O in 0 1; do
    d=$(mk t_pyopt$O $V); R=$d/GXEMUL-SEC/regress; cp "$R/pcc2_width_probe.py" "$R/zz-legit_probe.py"
    python3 - "$R/gate_hygiene.sh" <<'PY'
import io, re, sys
p = sys.argv[1]; s = io.open(p, encoding="utf-8", newline="").read()
m = re.search(r'(?ms)^GRANDFATHERED_PROMPTLESS="[^"]*"[ \t]*\n', s); assert m
s = s[:m.end()] + 'GRANDFATHERED_PROMPTLESS="gdrom_cond_probe.py"\n' + s[m.end():]
io.open(p, "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="").write(s)
PY
    if [ $O = 1 ]; then ( cd "$R" && PYTHONOPTIMIZE=1 GXROOT="$d" bash "$d/GXEMUL-SEC/tools/derive_hygiene_pins.sh" --grandfather zz-legit_probe.py ) > "$W/last.out" 2>&1; DRC=$?
    else drv "$d" --grandfather zz-legit_probe.py; fi
    cp "$W/last.out" "$OUT/t_pyopt${O}_$V.out"
    echo "t_pyopt$O     $V rc=$DRC  $(first)  | zz-legit in the file: $(grep -c 'zz-legit_probe.py' "$R/gate_hygiene.sh")" >> "$T"
  done
done
# t_gpbytes -- the gate's own name pins through the names' allow-list (glm: the M16 premise, never shown)
GPB=$(sed -n '/^GRANDFATHERED_PROMPTLESS="/,/"[[:space:]]*$/p' "$SEC/regress/gate_hygiene.sh" | sed 's/^GRANDFATHERED_PROMPTLESS="//; s/"[[:space:]]*$//' | tr -d ' \n' | LC_ALL=C tr -d 'A-Za-z0-9._/-' | wc -c)
GPN=$(sed -n '/^GRANDFATHERED_PROMPTLESS="/,/"[[:space:]]*$/p' "$SEC/regress/gate_hygiene.sh" | sed 's/^GRANDFATHERED_PROMPTLESS="//; s/"[[:space:]]*$//' | tr ' ' '\n' | grep -c .)
WFB=$(grep -E '^WHOLE_FULL_FILES=' "$SEC/regress/gate_hygiene.sh" | sed 's/^WHOLE_FULL_FILES="//; s/"$//' | tr -d '\n' | LC_ALL=C tr -d 'A-Za-z0-9._/: -' | wc -c)
echo "t_gpbytes    SEC gate: GRANDFATHERED_PROMPTLESS $GPN names, $GPB bytes outside the allow-list; WHOLE_FULL_FILES $WFB bytes outside it (+ ':' and space)" >> "$T"
rm -rf "$W"; echo "CONTROLS_C6D_DONE $(date '+%F %T')" >> "$T"; cat "$T" | tee -a "$OUT/SUMMARY_new.txt"

#!/bin/bash
# census F6d controls (c6d) under GIT-BASH: the report, --write (and the gate under WSL on the tree it wrote), the
# parser check at --grandfather / --release, and the writer-failure path -- F6d vs F6 (F6c too for the writer case).
# The work directory is PROJECT-LOCAL so WSL can reach the tree (git-bash maps $TEMP to /tmp, which WSL's /tmp is not:
# controls_c6b's corrections, item 5).  PREDICTIONS.txt (controls_c6d/) was written first.  Run from git-bash.
set -u
SEC=/c/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC
S5=/c/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/census0923
OUT=$SEC/regress/artifacts/hygienecensus_20260923/controls_c6d
W=$S5/gb6d_work; rm -rf "$W"; mkdir -p "$W"
RUL="2026-09-23: owner ruling for this control case only"
EW="ends""with"
mk() {
  local d=$W/$1_$2; mkdir -p "$d/GXEMUL-SEC/regress" "$d/GXEMUL-SEC/tools"
  (cd "$SEC/regress" && find . -maxdepth 1 -type f -exec cp -p {} "$d/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/" \;)
  case "$2" in
    F6d) cp -p "$SEC/tools/derive_hygiene_pins.sh" "$d/GXEMUL-SEC/tools/" ;;
    F6c) cp -p "$S5/derive_hygiene_pins.f6c.sh" "$d/GXEMUL-SEC/tools/derive_hygiene_pins.sh" ;;
    F6)  cp -p "$S5/derive_hygiene_pins.f6.sh" "$d/GXEMUL-SEC/tools/derive_hygiene_pins.sh"; cp -p "$S5/gate_hygiene.f6.sh" "$d/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/gate_hygiene.sh" ;;
  esac
  echo "$d"; }
drv() { local d=$1 o=$2; shift 2; ( GXROOT="$d" bash "$d/GXEMUL-SEC/tools/derive_hygiene_pins.sh" "$@" ) > "$o" 2>&1; DRC=$?; }
first() { grep -E '^(REFUSED|WRITTEN|DERIVE_FAIL|GRANDFATHERED:|RELEASED)' "$1" | head -1 | cut -c1-110; }
gfn() { sed -n '/^GRANDFATHERED_PROMPTLESS="/,/"[[:space:]]*$/p' "$1/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/gate_hygiene.sh" | tr ' "' '\n\n' | grep -c '\.py$'; }
{ echo "t_gitbash_c6d -- $(date '+%F %T'); F6d $(md5sum $SEC/tools/derive_hygiene_pins.sh | cut -c1-8); python3 rc $(python3 -c 'import sys' >/dev/null 2>&1; echo $?); python $(python -c 'import sys; print(sys.version.split()[0])' 2>&1 | head -1)"
  for V in F6d F6; do
    d=$(mk rep $V); drv "$d" "$OUT/t_gbreport_$V.out"
    echo "t_gbreport  $V rc=$DRC  $(grep -E '^DERIVE_(DONE|FAIL)' "$OUT/t_gbreport_$V.out" | head -1 | cut -c1-80)  CR bytes: $(tr -cd '\r' < "$OUT/t_gbreport_$V.out" | wc -c)  WHOLE_FULL_FILES got/pinned equal: $(grep -A1 '^WHOLE_FULL_FILES' "$OUT/t_gbreport_$V.out" | sed 's/.*\[\(.*\)\]/\1/' | uniq | wc -l | sed 's/^1$/yes/')"
    d=$(mk grand $V); R=$d/GXEMUL-SEC/regress; cp "$R/pcc2_width_probe.py" "$R/zz-legit_probe.py"
    drv "$d" "$OUT/t_gbgrand_$V.out" --grandfather zz-legit_probe.py
    echo "t_gbgrand   $V rc=$DRC  $(first "$OUT/t_gbgrand_$V.out")  | list $(gfn "$d") names"
    d=$(mk rel $V); R=$d/GXEMUL-SEC/regress
    printf 'if buf.rstrip().%s("GXemul>"): pass\n' "$EW" >> "$R/gdrom_cond_probe.py"
    drv "$d" "$OUT/t_gbrelease_$V.out" --release gdrom_cond_probe.py
    echo "t_gbrelease $V rc=$DRC  $(first "$OUT/t_gbrelease_$V.out")  | list $(gfn "$d") names"
    d=$(mk k14 $V); R=$d/GXEMUL-SEC/regress
    printf '\n\ndef _k14(x):\n    match x:\n        case K.%s("GXemul>"):\n            pass\n' "$EW" >> "$R/gdrom_cond_probe.py"
    drv "$d" "$OUT/t_gbk14_$V.out" --release gdrom_cond_probe.py
    echo "t_gbk14     $V rc=$DRC  $(first "$OUT/t_gbk14_$V.out")  | list $(gfn "$d") names"
    d=$(mk wr $V); R=$d/GXEMUL-SEC/regress; cp "$R/arm_flags_probe.py" "$R/zz_gbwhole_probe.py"
    drv "$d" "$OUT/t_gbwrite_$V.out" --write --ruling "$RUL"
    echo "t_gbwrite   $V rc=$DRC  $(first "$OUT/t_gbwrite_$V.out" | cut -c1-90)"
    echo "    pins: $(grep -E '^(EXPECT_PTY_PROBES|EXPECT_WHOLE_FULL|WHOLE_FULL_FILES)=' "$R/gate_hygiene.sh" | tr '\n' ' ')  CR bytes in the gate: $(tr -cd '\r' < "$R/gate_hygiene.sh" | wc -c)  bash -n: $(bash -n "$R/gate_hygiene.sh" 2>&1 && echo ok)"
    if [ $V = F6d ]; then
      WR=$(echo "$R" | sed 's#^/c/#/mnt/c/#')

1. **Q1 — closures and classifications**

   The narrow F6d shim repair is correct. I found a separate REAL-use failure described under Q2. Keep the exotic residuals as one consolidated LOW item.

   | Item | Assessment |
   |---|---|
   | N1 | Partial closure correctly disclosed. Token grammar and file-existence checks work; the remaining ambiguity requires crafted filenames. LOW is appropriate. |
   | N2 | Correct REAL-use fix. CR stripping addresses Windows Python output; saved Git Bash controls support it. |
   | N3 | Correct release safeguard for real probe names. Forced classifier faults remain exotic; the parser independently rejects the class-pattern case. |
   | N4 | Correct for glob expansion in all four printed lists. Crafted-name case; retaining the fix is reasonable. |
   | N5 | Correct isolation fix. The demonstrated exact canary-name collision is a constructed filename case under the owner’s rule. |
   | N6 | Correct **liveness** improvement. It does not establish classifier correctness. |
   | N7 | LOW disposition fits the deliberately broken-classifier controls. “The REPORT alone stays unproven” overstates protection: a failure confined to whole-mode classification need not produce any GONE names. |
   | N8 | Correct empty/nondecimal-value rejection. Numeric output still does not establish successful execution of its producer. Injected garbling remains an exotic control. |
   | N9 | Correct. LF and control-byte rulings are rejected; saved measurements support this. |
   | N10 | Correct for this snapshot. I independently checked the current names; introducing active shell bytes requires changing the gate outside these validated writers. LOW fits. |
   | N11 | Correct disclosure. Disabling assertions through `PYTHONOPTIMIZE` remains an unusual-environment case. |
   | N12 | Correctly filed for altered gate layouts. The delivered layout does not demonstrate that failure. |
   | N13 | Correctly narrowed historical statement. |
   | N14 | Correct removal of the claimed proof; subject to N7’s remaining overstatement. |
   | N15 | `astcall` supplies the missing release guard. M9’s comment-filter limitation can affect ordinary probe filenames; its disposition rests on the settled spelling-census scope, **not** on requiring an exotic filename. |
   | N16 | Correct fix for the shim swallowing Python’s nonzero status. It does **not** close all writer-failure-as-success cases: Q2 measures another. |

2. **Q2 — smallest REAL-use failure and denominator**

   **An ordinary `--write`, with unchanged valid pins and no ruling, can report success during disk exhaustion.**

   Both the [name writer](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/census0923/c6_frozen/derive_hygiene_pins.sh:256) and [pin writer](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/census0923/c6_frozen/derive_hygiene_pins.sh:314) use a temporary file object:

   ```python
   io.open(..., "w", ...).write(...)
   ```

   They never explicitly close it before printing success. A short write can leave the remaining bytes buffered. If flushing those bytes fails during object destruction, Python can suppress that exception and exit successfully.

   I executed the **unchanged extracted writers** in Python 3.12.10 subprocesses, using an in-memory filesystem-failure adapter and standard Python buffering:

   | Writer path | Complete write | Immediate ENOSPC | ENOSPC during final flush | Explicit-close control |
   |---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
   | `--write` | rc 0 | rc 1 | **rc 0, `WRITTEN`** | rc 1 |
   | `--grandfather` | rc 0 | rc 1 | **rc 0, `GRANDFATHERED`** | rc 1 |
   | `--release` | rc 0 | rc 1 | **rc 0, `RELEASED`** | rc 1 |

   That is **12 writer tests across all three mutation paths**. In the unchanged `--write` case, only **61,440 of 64,542 bytes** were retained despite `WRITTEN` and rc 0. F6d’s shell wrapper consequently has no nonzero status to detect and would print `DERIVE_DONE`.

   **Classification: REAL use.** A short write followed by ENOSPC models ordinary disk or quota exhaustion. It requires no crafted name, altered classifier, unusual environment variable, or malformed gate. Filesystem failure was injected; actual disk exhaustion and the complete shell invocation were not performed.

   **Required fix:** explicitly close both output files and propagate close errors before printing success. The explicit-close controls correctly failed.

   Separately, I enumerated all **104 non-artifact Python files**, finding:

   - **73** PTY-denominator files; **0/73** token/AST readiness disagreements or disallowed names.
   - **43** promptless files matching **43 unique** grandfathered names; NEW and GONE were both empty.
   - **76** whole-census candidates: the 73 plus `selftest_absorb.py`, `selftest_budget.py`, and `selftest_logdir.py`.
   - Exactly `arm_bdt_probe.py:1 arm_flags_probe.py:2`.
   - Textual counts `bare=0`, `allends=49`, `full=49`, `unknown=0`; converted counts `24/24/24`.
   - **0/104** AST parse failures.

3. **Q3 — shim and `astcall`**

   I found no additional defect in their argument forwarding, CRLF handling, or heredoc arrangement. Both `astcall` and `pywrite` bypass the shim correctly.

   Seven direct `astcall` controls returned the expected statuses: real promptless file **1**, real ready file **0**, real file with an added readiness call **0**, syntax error **2**, class pattern **1**, CRLF readiness code **0**, and missing path **2**. The shell deliberately skips `astcall` when releasing a deleted path.

   Fresh Git Bash and WSL execution was blocked by this review environment’s access restrictions. The supplied shell-control records support normal operation, but I do not count those as fresh executions by this seat.

4. **Q4 — records requiring correction**

   - The writer-failure assurance remains incomplete because of Q2.
   - Replace “the report alone stays unproven” with a statement that classifier failures can also compromise derived values; GONE is a particular guard, not a general success check.
   - The unchanged [classifier docstring](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/census0923/c6_frozen/census_tokens.py:20) still says `--release` removes a class-pattern-only probe. Mark that explicitly as historical; F6d now refuses it.
   - The [gate’s diagnostic](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/census0923/c6_frozen/gate_hygiene.sh:488) still directs users to edit `GRANDFATHERED_PROMPTLESS` manually. Direct them to the deriver instead.
   - F5/F6 result rows match, but their saved tables are not literally identical: the completion marker changes from `CONTROLS_C5_DONE` to `CONTROLS_C6_BASE_DONE`.

5. **Q5 — closing**

   **Not valid to close yet.** Diff delivery is sound: I verified all seven deriver hunks and both gate hunks against the frozen files, and all supplied MD5 prefixes match. The historical roster does not establish completion of the current panel; Kimi’s saved quota-error output is not a completed review.

   The acceptance measurement I need is the **late-write-failure control through the repaired shell entrypoints**, requiring nonzero exit status and no success marker for all three mutation paths under the installed shells. The operator’s already-owed final gate run remains outstanding; I did not run it.

   **Seat record:** GPT-6 / Codex. Read the entire brief, frozen deriver and classifier, gate readiness section, corresponding F5 diffs, and relevant control drivers/results. Ran independent enumeration, token/AST comparisons, byte-grammar checks, seven parser controls, and twelve writer subprocess controls. All new write tests used memory; no gate, battery, or harness was run.
